* All meetings, except closed sessions, are open to the public.  The Board welcomes citizens’ comments.  The public participation 
portion of the meeting is limited to one -half hour for discussion of agenda items and one -half hour for non -agenda items.  Each 
person will be allowed to speak for up to three minutes, except where the number of speakers exceeds the ti me limit.  In those 
instances, the President may reduce the time limit for each speaker in order to accommodate all of the individuals who wish t o 
address the Board of Education.  In any event, no one’s right to address the Board will be denied.

A. CAPE (Citizens and Parents for Education) Steering Committee 
     for Bond 2013 
At this time, we would like to personally thank our CAPE Steering 
Committee who volunteered countless hours of their time to work on 
the District’s 2013 Bond Proposal.  Through their dedication and hard 
work, the bond proposal was a success!   
Tonight, we are pleased to recognize Tracy Heikka, Jen Hilzinger, Dave 
Lambert, and Raj Thangamuthu,  the CAPE Steering Committee for 
their exemplary service.

 President Philippart called the meeting to order at 7:00 
p.m. 
In addition to Ms. Philippart, present were Board 
members Edmunds, Fleming, Hauff, Miletti, Schmidt, and 
Underwood. Two high school student representatives 
were also in attendance.  Also present were 
Superintendent Fowler; administrators Birmingham, 
Keeling, Machesky, Mull, Rajter, Washington, and Witt; 
and approximately 200 additional members of the 
audience.

Shawna Haras, Schroeder parent, addressed the Board 
about the recognition the Troy School District has 
received for high rankings in  education by Family Circle, 
Money Magazine, and U.S. News.  She talked about the 
volume of curriculum that mu st be taught in classrooms 
today along with cultural diversity and special needs 
involved and added that she is very concerned there is no 
teacher contract yet.   
Cindy Shankin, Troy teacher and member of the TEA 
bargaining team, addressed the Board to request that 
bargaining sessions with the Dist rict be held after school 
hours.  She mentioned that the last session was during 
the school day and teacher reps had to be out of the 
classroom.  She asked that the Board consider scheduling 
sessions outside of the school day.   
Steve Dunneback, Royal Oak resident and parent of a 
Troy student, addressed the Board regarding teacher 
negotiations.  He indicated that he would support the 
bond because the money is needed for updates to the 
buildings and cannot be used for compensation.  He 
talked about the District’s operating fund balance, the 
issues regarding public education in Lansing, increased 
enrollment in the district and teachers’ pay freeze and 
step increases.   
Diane Loucks, Troy High teacher, parent, and member of 
the TEA bargaining team, addressed the Board regarding 
negotiations.  She talked about the District preserving its 
standard of excellence and th e need to invest in the

Jennifer Lee, Troy teacher, addressed the Board 
future – in its teachers.  She provided copies of a chart 
providing an overview of teacher salaries and salaries

Jayne Kelly, Troy parent and Wass teacher, read a letter 
written by her husband, Brian, who could not be in 
attendance.  Mr. Kelly’s letter talked about step increases 
for teachers and his concern fo r teachers looking for jobs 
in other districts.   
Tony Lucchi addressed the Board regarding the ongoing 
teacher contract negotiations and talked about the 
comparison of Troy Schools to surrounding districts.  He 
spoke briefly about performance-based compensation.   
Laurie Pickell, Martell teacher, addressed the Board about 
teachers working without a contract and how they give 
over 100% and put their heart and soul into teaching 
their students.  She talked about loss of compensation for 
teachers and mentioned the salary of the HR director and 
the Business director.   
Charlie Tull, Wass parent and Larson teacher, wife 
teaches kindergarten at Troy Union, and taxpayer in the 
community.  He talked about the status of negotiations 
and that the District and TEA should be working together 
and respect each other as team mates would.  He 
mentioned that relationships and honesty are needed in 
the process in order to be successful.    
Christine Russell, Larson and Hill parent, Troy teacher 
since 2009.  She indicated she took a large pay cut when 
leaving Detroit Public Schools to come to Troy.  She also 
indicated that at this point she would qualify for Free & 
Reduced lunches if her husband lost his job.  She asked 
that steps be given back to teachers. 
Ben Martin, Troy teacher fo r 14 years, addressed the 
Board regarding the lack of a teacher contract and 
inquired as to how teacher salaries are compared to 
other districts.  
Alan Harnisch, Troy resident, addressed the Board about 
the excellent TSD education and how hard the teachers 
work.  He shared that he is an attorney and has 
negotiated contracts with Fr anklin Village Police.  He 
talked about respect and that teachers are as important 
as police and asked the Board to settle the contract. 
Laurie Harnisch, reading recovery teacher at Schroeder, 
taught in Troy since 1986 and shared that they moved 
here for the exemplary schools.  She talked about

Negotiations Update 
Mr. Jasen Witt, Assistant Superintendent of Human 
Resources, spoke to the Board about the current status 
of negotiations between the District and the TEA.  He 
mentioned that the last tw o sessions were conducted 
stated that the legislative changes in Lansing have 
affected teachers, support staf f, administrators and other 
employees and that the District will continue to work 
toward finding an agreement.  He indicated that the 
difficult financial realities and problems with state funding 
have not gone away and that innovative solutions need 
to be found.  He added that during the last mediation 
session it was requested that details not be discussed in 
a public setting and that the Board would be able to 
discuss this further in closed  session.  He indicated that 
the next bargaining session would be held this Friday,

Ms. Underwood mentioned the request made by Cindy 
Shankin during the public communication portion of the 
meeting about conducting future bargaining sessions 
after or before school so that teachers do not have to be 
outside of their classroom during the school day.  Mr. 
Witt stated that they try to look at all options but have to 
coordinate the schedule with the mediator.  Ms. 
Underwood also asked about the mention of finding 
administrative salary information on the District website.  
Mr. Witt said it was available within the transparency 
report on the homepage.   
Mr. Schmidt mentioned the tenor of the conversations 
with the union and whether or not there has been any 
suggestion on changing the format of the meetings.  Mr. 
Witt stated that the union received a full economic offer 
and there has been some mo vement but not enough and 
they are working to build off of that.  He also mentioned 
that the union presented an offer, which was tied to per 
pupil funding.   
Ms. Philippart asked about data for salary comparisons.  
Mr. Witt responded that a number of other districts were 
reviewed and that some did give half-step increments 
and agreed to additional PD time.

2013 Bond Update 
Annual Report and Goals 
It was moved by Mr. Miletti and supported by Mrs. 
Fleming that be it therefore resolved  that the Troy 
Dr. Fowler provided a brief update on the bond campaign 
Dr. Fowler, Superintendent of Schools, provided the 
of the Troy School District, which is: “The purpose of the 
Troy School District is to ensure learning for all members 
of the school community.”  She reviewed the District 
goals for 2012/2013 which were as follows:   
1. Increase the number of students who are college and 
career ready in literacy, mathematics, science, and 
social studies;   
2. Continue interventions to raise the achievement level 
of lowest performing subgroups;  
3. Prepare for November 2013 Bond Election; and 
4. Continue and finalize data processing review. 
She mentioned the new account ability color-code system 
that districts are expected to have 85% of all students 
proficient on state assessments by 2021/2022. The 
2012/2013 MEAP assessment data for grades 3 – 8 and 
Michigan Merit Exam results were also reviewed.  She 
then provided an overview of the Superintendent’s Goals 
for the 2013/2014 school year, which will be reported to 
the Board each quarter: 
1. Business Services 
2. Human Resources 
3. Bond Election 
4. Student Achievement 
5. Community/Business Development  
She reviewed the MEAP reading, math, and science 
percent proficient trajectory for grades 3 – 8 as well as 
the school scorecard status and MME math and reading 
scores.

SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved By: ______________________________ 
Supported By: ______________________________ 
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED that the Troy School District’s Board of 
Education intends to continue summer tax collections for the 20 14-2015 fiscal 
year and will notify the City of Troy of this action and that a copy of the attached 
resolution be incorporated as part of the official minutes of the Board of 
Education. 
Yes: ____________ 
No: ____________ 
109
     8. A.        11.12.13 
TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
TROY, MICHIGAN 48098 
ANNUAL SUMMER TAX RESOLUTION 
 WHEREAS, this Board of Ed ucation by resolution of November 12, 
2013, determined to impose a summer property tax levy to collect one -half of 
school property taxes, including debt services, upon property located within the 
Troy School District, beginning with 20 14 and continuing from year to year until 
specifically revoked by this Board of Education. 
 NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 1.  This Board of Education, pursuant to 1976 P .A. 451, as amended, 
hereby invokes a summer tax levy of one -half of school property taxes, including 
debt service, continuing with 20 14, and continuing from year to year until 
specifically revoked by this Board of Education, and requests each city in which 
this district is located and in which a local school district or city is concurrently 
imposing a summer tax levy to collect those summer taxes. 
 2.  The Superintendent or his/her designee is authorized and directed 
to forward to the governing body of each city in which this district is located and 
in which a local school district or city is concurrently imposing a summer tax levy, 
a copy of this Board’s resolution requesting that each such city agree to coll ect 
the summer tax levy for 20 14 in the amount specified in this resolution.  Such 
forwarding of the resolution and the request to collect the summer tax levy shall 
be sent so that they are received by the appropriate governi ng bodies before 
January 1, 2014. 
 3.  The Superintendent or his/her designee is authorized and directed 
to negotiate on behalf of this District with the governing body of each city in 
which the District is located for the reasonable expenses for collection of the 
District’s summer tax levy that the city may bill und er MCLA 380.1612.  Any such 
proposed agreement shall be brought before this Board for its approval or 
disapproval. 
 4.  All resolutions and parts of resolutions insofar as they conflict with 
the provisions of this resolution be and the same are hereby rescinded. 
 Yes: Members 
 No: Members 
110
     8. A.        11.12.13 
Resolution declared adopted.  
_____________________________Secretary, Board of Education 
 The undersigned, duly qualified and acting Secretary of the Board of 
Education of Troy School District, Troy, Michigan, hereby certifies that the 
foregoing constitutes a true and complete copy of a resolution adopted by said 
Board of Education at a regular meeting held on November 12, 2013, the original 
of which is part of the Board’s minutes.  The undersigned further certi fies that 
notice of the meeting was given to the public pursuant to the provisions of the 
“Open Meetings Act” (Act 267, P.A. 1976, as amended).
